Add opt-in markov_shuffle / balanced_transitions to MarkovConsumerType (stacked on the distributions PR)

4cdd9ad38e9ed6e1b62caf5756e278247d592807f4b154ed40010acc35e0dd2f97cadefc46d78e58Alan Lujanalanlujan91@gmail.com2026-08-11T18:01:46-04:00Give each commit on main its own CI concurrency group

cancel-in-progress: false was not sufficient. GitHub permits one running
plus one PENDING run per concurrency group, and a third arrival discards
the pending one, so a run of merges leaves most commits on main with no
build at all. Measured: run 31536842419 for 828680ad was created and
cancelled ten minutes later having allocated zero jobs, and three
consecutive main commits went the same way while the earlier fix was
already in place.

The failure mode is worse than missing coverage, because such a run
reports conclusion 'cancelled', which reads as a build that ran and was
interrupted rather than one that never started. That is how a real
test_economy failure on ubuntu-3.10 stayed unreported for hours.

Keying the group on the sha for main means no run on main is ever
superseded. Feature branches keep one group per ref and still supersede,
which is what you want while iterating on a PR.

Make CubicHermiteInterp deepcopy/pickle independent of scipy spline internals
24f32cb168934f2d0786717727032c87cf3dc6c70fc0422153fed77de0909c7ef9f4e0cda06216f0Alan Lujanalanlujan91@gmail.com2026-08-11T16:44:32-04:00Resolve changelog merges with the union driver

Every branch appends a bullet to the same section of docs/CHANGELOG.md,
so nearly every pull request conflicts there and the correct resolution
is always to keep both bullets. git's built-in union driver does that
automatically and needs no local configuration.

Hand resolution has not just been tedious, it has been wrong: diff3
conflicts carry a fourth marker, '||||||| <sha>', beyond the usual three,
and two of those were committed to this file on main earlier today.

0fc0422153fed77de0909c7ef9f4e0cda06216f07c957d8d428e3bb298ca9a4963d9c1c7c8ec2e4dAlan Lujanalanlujan91@gmail.com2026-08-11T16:27:00-04:00Align supported Python with SPEC 0 and stop cancelling main's build

SPEC 0 drops a Python version three years after release, so the window
is now 3.12, 3.13 and 3.14; 3.11 left it in October 2025. numpy and
scipy both already require >=3.12, so the 3.10 and 3.11 jobs were
resolving a dependency stack about two years old and testing a
configuration HARK is not developed against, rather than giving extra
coverage of HARK on older Python.

- requires-python >=3.12, classifiers updated to 3.12/3.13/3.14.
- hark.yml sweeps the window on Linux and covers 3.13 on macOS and
  Windows: 5 jobs rather than 12.
- fail-fast: false. One failing cell used to cancel the other eleven,
  which leaves a single data point and invites the wrong diagnosis.
- cancel-in-progress no longer applies to main. Consecutive merges were
  cancelling each other, so main's build almost never concluded and a
  real test failure went unreported for hours.
- examples.yml follows the same window; execute-notebooks.yml drops a
  3.10 pin justified by a numba limitation from 2023 that no longer
  holds; weekly.yml moves off 3.11.

numba and llvmlite publish cp312/cp313/cp314 wheels, and the remaining
dependencies are pure Python, so 3.14 installs cleanly.

7c957d8d428e3bb298ca9a4963d9c1c7c8ec2e4d83e22d9819d5d5cee1f7020783bc54998e9e1586Alan Lujanalanlujan91@gmail.com2026-08-11T16:26:19-04:00Revert the shuffle remainder fix; it moves a Krusell-Smith golden

The fix is correct: draw(shuffle=True) allocated leftover slots in
proportion to pmv rather than to the fractional remainders, so
E[count_j] != N*pmv[j]. But ConsAggShockModel calls it, so correcting
the allocation changes the simulated economy, and
testAggShockMarkovConsumerType::test_economy moves AFunc[0].slope from
1.05654 to 1.06030. Verified by control: that test passes at cd2b7ea6
and fails with the fix applied.

AFunc[0].slope is an aggregate law-of-motion coefficient. Changing it
belongs in a reviewed pull request where the new value is visible, not
in a direct push, so this goes back and returns as a PR.

83e22d9819d5d5cee1f7020783bc54998e9e15868efb8625dfe0b7378240cc68c7700b3e6cffe3c1Alan Lujanalanlujan91@gmail.com2026-08-11T15:53:15-04:00Fix biased remainder allocation in DiscreteDistribution.draw(shuffle=True)

After allocating floor(N*pmv) slots per atom, the M leftover slots were
distributed using Q = K_exact - (1/N)*K. That mixes units: K_exact is in
slot units and K/N is in probability units, and since K/N is approximately
pmv, it is negligible against N*pmv. Q therefore collapsed to pmv, so the
leftovers went out in proportion to pmv rather than to the fractional
remainders, and E[count_j] != N*pmv[j].

Correcting Q to K_exact - K is necessary but not sufficient: the loop
consumed Q one slot at a time and zeroed each atom after use, which is
successive sampling, whose inclusion probabilities are not proportional
to the sizes. That remains biased whenever M >= 2. The remainder is now
allocated by systematic sampling from a single uniform offset, which
makes P(atom j gets an extra slot) exactly Q[j].

The error is O(M/N), so it vanishes for population-sized draws and every
existing caller sat in that regime. It is severe at the cohort sizes
sim_birth redraws: with pmv = [0.7, 0.2, 0.1] the realized long-run
marginal at N=3 was 0.872/0.086/0.042 against a target of 0.7/0.2/0.1.
Measured unbiased after the fix across 27 configurations, max |z| = 2.1.

draw(0, shuffle=True) also raised, via ZeroDivisionError on 1.0/N for a
python int and an IndexError on a float64 empty index array for np.int64.
sim_birth asks for zero draws in any period with no deaths, so this was
reachable in ordinary simulation. Both paths now return an empty array.

Both new tests fail against the previous implementation.

aa92b8ea1fcf50ccfbd0b2b64242a32d31ed55b2f46b97ba854d9a2618ae8c24be0f73d566ef58c9Matthew N. Whitemnwhite@gmail.com2020-03-22T05:28:28+05:30Improve ConsPortfolioModel (#577)

* Make risky share functions update as a method

Previously had to pass constructed inputs to PortfolioConsumerType in parameter dictionary; now just pass RiskyAvg and RiskyStd, and attributes are constructed as part of update().  Live programming with Mridul!

* Portfolio model can handle age-varying risk beliefs

ConsPortfolioModel previously assumed that risky asset has same profile at all ages.  Now a type can *believe* that risky asset has different distribution depending on age by making RiskyAvg and RiskyStd be lists.  However, this belief is obviously false; user must also specify singleton values of RiskyAvgTrue and RiskyStdTrue to use in simulation.

* clean up if else logic

* allow RiskyAvg and RiskyStd to be either a list of a numpy array

* cast Risky* to list if they are arrays, time_vary expects them to be lists

* Adjust comments, remove math import

A few comments have been adjusted and added, and all references to the math package have been replaced with numpy.

* Add portfolio dictionary to ConsumerParameters

ConsumerParameters.py now has a default dictionary for ConsPortfolioModel.  It is solved in PortfolioPerceptions.py, along with the previous age-varying risk perceptions type.  Will rename that file on the next commit.

* Rename to PortfolioConsumerType.py

Just a single file rename, as this file is now structured like the other model example files.

* PortfolioConsumerType now has default parameters

User can now make a default PortfolioConsumerType by passing null parameters.  Still has to specify cycles, like our other dictionaries.  Need to check if we can fix that.

* Simplify/update ConsPortfolioModel tests

Old test for ConsPortfolioModel created a dictionary inside the test.  Now uses the default dictionary.  Also updated RiskyShare targets in the test.

* Move initialization code into updateX methods

PortfolioConsumerType did a bunch of work directly in its init method, which is not how any other AgentType subclass worked.  Everything has now been shifted into updateX methods, which are called from update.  The check that BoroCnstArt has been put into the solver itself.

* Remove the redundant LogNormalPortfolioConsumerType

The bits that were in LogNormalPortfolioConsumerType have been moved into PortfolioConsumerType, so this small subclass is now redundant.

* First pass at reworked ConsPortfolioModel

Redid the solver for ConsPortfolioModel from scratch.  Much shorter, simpler code.  Only small speedup.  Still need to add value function constructor and test AdjustPrb < 1.

* Slight revision

Fixed lower extrapolation of dvdsFuncFxd.  Need to talk with CDC to see if he has math intuition on this.  Committing to change computers.

* Simulation methods in revised portfolio model work

Everything seems to work properly.

* Revert parameters to fix broke test

ShareCount should be 26, not 25, but the test assumes 25.  Likewise, test is based on RiskyCount being 5, which seems small with a stdev of 0.2.  But okay.

* Minor fixes to new solver

One typo in definition of Rxs. Also previously omitted LivPrb, now fixed.

Also adjusted example plot of RiskyShareFunc in examples/PortfolioConsumerType.  I didn't notice that PKM's ShareFunc is defined on aNrm, not mNrm.

* New portfolio model version can construct vFunc

Added section of code to make vFuncFxd and vFuncAdj in ConsPortfolioModelALT.  Seems to work as intended.

Also reverted a couple more dictionary parameters to get that stupid test to pass.  I'll change the target value later, once we settle on what the default parameters should be.

* New portfolio model correctly handles IncUnemp=0

Previous commits had (anticipated) bug if zero income is possible, creating duplicate gridpoints at zero.  Now fixed on this commit.

* Value function representation uses CubicInterp

Matching the style of other value functions in HARK, the pseudo-inverse value function for the revised portfolio model now uses cubic splines.

* New portfolio solver can optimize on discrete Share

Added option to choose risky portfolio share discretely (on ShareGrid), rather than continuously (solving FOC-s).  Near-discontinuities in ShareFunc are put at the midway point b/w consecutive mNrm gridpoints (of the cFunc).

* Implemented faster expectations in new portfolio model

If IncomeDstn and RiskyDstn are independent, then expectations over shocks can be computed sequentially.  This speeds up the code by roughly a factor of RiskyCount.  The solver can also correctly handle non-independent distributions, but as is the update method makes them independent (and sets the appropriate flag).

I've discovered that the solver becomes unstable when UpdatePrb is low, like 0.1.  It works for 0.15.  Not sure what's going on.

* Begin conversion to new dictionary format

Seb has converted existing models to have their default dictionary in the model file itself.  This commit breaks the new version of the portfolio model, as I need to merge his branch into this one.

* Put dictionary in old portfolio model file

Tests can't even load/build HARK because the old CPM file wanted to load ConsumerParameters, which has now been deleted.  This commit tries to fix that.

* Move PortfolioConsumerType.py content to new location

The example file in examples/ConsPortfolioModel/PortfolioConsumerType.py was a better fit for examples/ConsumptionSaving/example_ConsPortfolioModel.py.  It's now been moved there.

* Add examples file for new portfolio model version

Removed "if main" block from bottom of ConsPortfolioModelALT.py.  Added example file showing the scope of the model.

* ConsPortfolioModelALT.py add MertSam limit to plots

* Improved parallelNelderMead

Made some fixes to HARK.parallel.parallelNelderMead:

1) Renamed maxcores to maxthreads, to be more correct
2) Fixed various small comments
3) Applied fix if degree of parallelization is illegal
4) Fixed computation of x_dist (whoops)
5) Allowed initial simplex to be saved to disk
6) Adjusted save/load procedure to use comma as delimiter rather than space (and use one line per simplex point), to make it easier to copy-paste points from the simplex file while estimation is in progress.

* fix linting changes

Prepare release 0.10.0.dev3
9c20b863dfbe8e0c398a9995315b38cee24d42d93ed97e4fa298c0984211f0f6df2e911e8b53a705 64266fbc422ffd989f7cda01db47f768857971a3Matthew N. Whitemnwhite@gmail.com2019-05-16T09:14:10-04:00Merge pull request #282 from keithblaha/validators

Add non_empty argument validator
3ed97e4fa298c0984211f0f6df2e911e8b53a70506f1a26879fbf482deb98ca3bc1b82b2c062ebe9 3f4da42c68da105cfdda382b9067e884a8b93cf7Matthew N. Whitemnwhite@gmail.com2019-05-15T17:50:19-04:00Merge pull request #287 from econ-ark/Multithreading-fix-maybe

Chris and I discovered that HARK.parallel.multithreadCommands did not work on some of our project code... but only in some Python environments. On Windows, I could only generate this error on Python 3, but everything worked correctly on Python 2. On Mac and/or Linux, Chris found that the error came up when running Python in a terminal, but not when running Spyder; he also tested various web servers.

After some digging, Chris found that recent versions of joblib (which multithreadCommands uses) changed the default backend of joblib from multiprocessing to loky. Apparently something in loky does not play nicely with our class structure, as it can't (de)serialize at least some AgentType subclasses.

This fixes the issue by simply changing the backend argument on the call to Parallel. It also fixes one typo in a comment.

8e453dead4b6303e9fbd0632c8d845bed9adedccffb501242b59603bed860d4300d8261232dd7428Christopher Llorracc Carroll1320319+llorracc@users.noreply.github.com2019-04-08T21:06:15-04:00Changed hardcoded updateAFunc parameters into proper parameters (#244)

* Changed hardcoded updateAFunc parameters into proper parameters

Four parameters that govern how CobbDouglasEconomy.updateAFunc works
were defined locally, within the method, but are now attributes to be
assigned at init (or at least before the user tries to solve):

- update_weight --> DampingFac, now defined complementarily
- verbose --> verbose
- discard_periods --> T_discard
- max_loops --> max_loops

To prevent this from being a breaking change, init method writes old
hardcoded values if omitted from passed inputs.  This will be improved
with a warning later.

Untested, as it turns out Anaconda3 is incorrectly installed on this
computer.

* Tested de-hardcoded AFunc updating parameters

Put new parameters in dictionaries in ConsumerParameters.py.  Also added necessary lines to MarkovCobbDouglasEconomy and fixed one output description.

* Check Impatience Conditions

A method that checks 6 conditions, which may be sufficient for a
nondegenerate solution.

* Corrections

All parameters are of the instance’s type. A check for infinite horizon
models with a 1 period cycle is also added.

* Adding [0] for a list variable

PermGroFac[0]

* Corrections

GIC, WRIC, and FVAC are evaluated by IndShockConsumerType. The code is
restructured to evaluate one condition at a time in a block. The method
is renamed to checkConditions. Extra indentation is avoided.

exp_psi_to_one_minus_rho is omitted from PerfForesightConsumerType.
Returns feedback is changed with none in the docstrings. Default
verbosity is false.

df72f65797fb663e18638c6855cf711fad54e1dc91697d33ab2e2641cb921fb458279e80a2a32db7dclowlow.david.chester@gmail.com2017-04-19T12:37:49-04:00Tiny fix to decay interpolation in linear interpolation in HARKinterpolation.py (#110)

91697d33ab2e2641cb921fb458279e80a2a32db7cf5650689a26b9c36d0a60404ebf143887078423Matthew N. Whitemnwhite@gmail.com2017-04-12T17:04:21-04:00Rewrote LinearInterp from scratch (#107)

* Rewrote LinearInterp from scratch

LinearInterp was previously mostly an extension of
scipy.interpolate.UnivariateSpline.  It has now been rewritten as a
fully custom class for HARK.  This was done because UnivariateSpline
uses 6-7x more memory than is necessary.  This is not a problem in
isolation, but can lead to memory constraints being binding in very
large models-- those with tens of thousands of LinearInterps.

All code runs identically as before these changes.  Old version remains
as LinearInterpOLD, for testing.  Should be deleted.  Some applications
run 5% faster, others are up to 14% slower than before.  We can fix
speed later by adding an option to the class that makes it use 50% more
memory.

Also fixed all classes where distance_criteria was not a static
attribute.

* Some minor suggested changes to Matts LinearInterp class.  This commit reorganizes the method so there is only one (_evalOrDer) that actually does anything.  The other methods just call _evalOrDer.  This avoids the repetition of code that was in the previous version.  Tested some and seems to work (and perhaps be a bit faster), though it needs to be tested more thoroughly with the rest of HARK.

cf5650689a26b9c36d0a60404ebf1438870784231ba21d3b9e1cf584e40ed9ae058f772a81b1d72cMatthew N. Whitemnwhite@gmail.com2017-04-11T18:39:12-04:00Fix to approxLognormal (#108)

approxLognormal previously didn't work properly at very extreme inputs.
For example, trying approxLognormal(N=20,mu=-26,sigma=15) results in
many of the discrete values being 0.  In some cases, all but the top
point were 0.  This commit fixes this issue by adding an alternate
version for extremely low shocks.  The math has been simplified a bit,
and a typo in the docstring removed.
1ba21d3b9e1cf584e40ed9ae058f772a81b1d72c90c026e3705bf6173004b4cd5b7de310e57ec9d1Matthew N. Whitemnwhite@gmail.com2017-01-13T09:28:13-05:00Fix issue #100

Pavel Solis pointed out in #100 that the KinkedR solver breaks when
Rboro == Rsave.  This is due to duplicative interpolation points, which
breaks LinearInterp and causes nonsense output.

The fix is to put a conditional around the offending lines, which makes
the solver act just like ConsIndShockSolver.  I added some comments to
this effect; also moved the Rboro >= Rsave assert to be with the
CubicBool assert for aesthetics.

41d3f1353f7075a53be662854d620795520ba53a939cf55bdf9100f43d7166bd1cd67a7718d0c293Matthew N. Whitemnwhite@gmail.com2016-07-13T14:55:40-04:00MedShock terminal solution: expenditure revision

MedShock model has problem with consumers violating budget constraint
due to convexity of cFunc at the bottom (combined with Med defined off
of cFunc from FOC).  Makes value function calculation impossible.

Fix: Interpolate over "total expenditure" function xFunc, then feed xLvl
into another interpolation to get optimal cLvl and Med, strictly obeying
cLvl+MedPrice*Med = xLvl.  Now implemented for terminal period only,
will extend to other periods in near future.

1de65df9133e5b493a4b96823d5352546f651682483fdb3604d2a5df1729ca73e0387d7db2b9f7e5Matthew N. Whitemnwhite@gmail.com2016-07-11T13:48:44-04:00Fixed med terminal, added med simulation

The terminal solution in the medical shocks model has been adjusted
correctly.  Takes nearly as long to solve terminal period solution as
non-terminal period, which is worrisome because a version of the former
must be done each period in the presence of a binding borrowing
constraint.

Added simulation routines for medical shock model; reinforces that we
need another simulation overhaul.  Test runs show that agents
occasionally (like, 1 in a million or less) violate their budget
constraint when they get a very bad medical shock.  This is a result of
badly capturing the *convexity* of the consumption function at the very
bottom.  Might need denser grid at bottom... or switch to cubic splines.

Programmed some of a bicubic interpolation method, untested.  Wrote
constructor and interpolation part, but there are *eight* extrapolation
routines to write.  Ugh.

087cf8deccaa9a5a4d67db5cabc2f1910280d86e0b32054d73dcdd0ffbb0148c5e9a992172d3ad81Matthew N. Whitemnwhite@gmail.com2016-07-07T11:08:49-04:00Simulation for persistent shocks works

Small updates to get persistent shock model simulation working.  Note
that the "naturally constrained consumption function" is a
HARKinterpolator2D with an empty distance_criteria ... This means that
convergence in an infinite horizon model *will not* depend on this
portion of the consumption function, only the "artificially constrained"
portion and the unconstrained portion.

2a343fbd64402e227c4f61752866bdae5d0a331ad1d861b9fdb46ac223b2c7f2fd242a14be2ff514Matthew N. Whitemnwhite@gmail.com2016-05-31T15:51:56-04:00Fixed cstwMPC lifecycle and FashionVictim

d1d861b9fdb46ac223b2c7f2fd242a14be2ff514432c70ff89bf2156faf33447e2d38ed393c9deb8Matthew N. Whitemnwhite@gmail.com2016-05-30T17:06:22-04:00GenEq-dev (#38)

* First draft of general gen eq solver

HARKcore.py now has a Market class, the core of the "general general
equilibrium solver".  Completely untested at this point, but its
docstring describes what the user must supply; will be further described
in the User Guide.

Next step: writing a baby gen eq model.  Possibly not one of any
economic interest, will brainstorm.

* Gen eq test model: FashionVictim

The general gen eq solver now has a "baby" test model to work with,
located in the /FashionVictim folder.  It is a model of dynamic binary
fashion choice, inspired by the paper, "Why Do All Hipsters Look The
Same?".  The Market.solve() method can find a dynamic equilibrium of
this model for some parameters, but convergence of the dynamic rule
doesn't always happen.

The old approxMeanOneLognormal (commented out) has now been deleted, as
it's just wasted space.

The AgentType class now has seed and RNG as standard attributes, along
with the resetRNG() method.  Going to incorporate this into existing
applications on next commit.

* Simulation overhaul

The ConsumptionSavingModel.py simulation routines have been completely
overhauled / replaced.  This includes both generating histories of
shocks and simulating histories of state and control variables.

The new approach is more "object oriented" and uses methods of
ConsumerType rather than a weird mix of methods and functions.

All of the old functions and methods still exist, as they are still used
by cstwMPC (SolvingMicroDSOPs uses the new approach).  These will be
deleted once I update cstwMPC appropriately.

Shock generation now draws directly on the discrete approximations in
IncomeDstn, rather than actually drawing lognormal shocks (and replacing
some with unemployment events).  This is a "safe" method that prevents
simulations from going awry if an agent experiences a (very unlikely)
sequence of simulated shocks that was unanticipated when solving the
model.

Four of the example types now do test simulations if the user wants:
LifecycleType, InfiniteType, KinkyType, and CyclicalType.  I will write
a Markov simulator soon, along with something to handle perfect
foresight consumers.

* Quick interpolation fix

Lower extrapolation on 1D interp methods now defaults to NaN, can revert
to linear lower extrap with lower_extrap=True.

* Three interpolation improvements

1) LinearInterp now has the option for "decay extrapolation" above the
range of x-points, just like CubicInterp.  Consumption functions now
respect perfect foresight solutions even when linear interpolation is
used.

2) CubicInterp was partially rewritten, resulting both in simpler code
and a significant speedup-- maybe 3x or more?

3) LowerEnvelope has been rewritten entirely so that it's no longer a
lie.  This was formerly ConstrainedComposite but was renamed during
Namingway, with no change in functionality.  Now it does what it says on
the tin: you pass it any number of 1D functions, it makes a function
that always returns the lowest among them.

* New simulation methods incorporated into cstwMPC

cstwMPC now uses the new simulation methods.  Infinite horizon results
closely match previous results, but lifecycle is a bit off; must
investigate.

Also put simulated mortality into the infinite horizon version, but this
doesn't play nicely with the current aggregation system.

* cstwMPC aggregate shocks model working

The (FBS) aggregate shocks version of the cstwMPC model has been
incorporated into cstwMPC.py, using the gen-eq framework in HARKcore.
Still might be some small bugs, will investigate.

New file: ConsAggShock.py, which has a basic solver for the aggregate
shocks model.  Pretty nifty at 70 lines long (half of which is comments
and whitespace).

HARKcore.py now has HARKobject, a supersuperclass with the
assignParameters (as __call__) and distance methods.  Some code can be
simplified with this, will be done during commenting and cleanup.

Passing a seed in HARKsimulation.py is now optional, and approxLognormal
handles sigma=0 correctly again.

* cstwMPC sensitivity analysis encapsulated

The sensitivity analysis in cstwMPC was a repetitive mess before... and
was also non-functional after recent changes.  It now has a function to
automate the analysis.  Output file names have changed, need to re-run
(and change the figure-generating scripts), but I don't have three days
to spare.  Later.

* Markov solver OO-ified

The Markov solver has is now more object oriented (and is better
commented).  The solve method reads fairly conceptually, calling a lot
of submethods.  This commit incorporates (in spirit) the commits to
DCL-dev-1 beyond its last merge to master.

* Markov simulator, OO pref shock model

ConsumptionSavingModel.py can now simulate Markov models (with
limitations), and generates proper histories of income shocks.

ConsPrefShockModel.py has been made object-oriented, inheriting from the
KinkedR solver.  Value function construction works.  Also has an OO
simulator.

Also made some small notation changes to cstwMPC.

87d9183bfafcd10e61c4e7d143c7dd2e6503d10bf1964a2c5a47cc43cc6b036575721acf8aac331aMatthew N. Whitemnwhite@gmail.com2016-05-12T17:01:07-04:00Multithread demo works again

f1964a2c5a47cc43cc6b036575721acf8aac331a4e199bb1fd6e0741080e108b8c6ee599823cdc60Matthew N. Whitemnwhite@gmail.com2016-05-11T17:34:53-04:00MNW-dev-3 (#37)

* Changed function names in original modules

All agreed on function/class name changes have been made *only* in the
files where they are defined.  Absolutely no module should work at this
point.

* Nearly all run-able files running after func name changes

Everything except TBSexamples and MultithreadDemo are working after
fixing class and function names.  MultithreadDemo seems to have a
conflict with OOP ConsumptionSavingModel; TBS reveals small oversight in
MarkovSolver, maybe.

* Most variable names changed, all run-able

Most consumption-saving model parameter names have been changed, and all
files have been updated to run-ability: ConsumptionSavingModel, cstwMPC,
TBSexamples, ConsPrefShockModel, SolvingMicroDSOPs.

Some "internal" variables still need to be changed, and there are a few
unchanged parameter names.  Advanced functionality of cstwMPC and
SolvingMicroDSOPs is untested.

* Most internal variables changed

Updated most the "internal" variables of all consumption-saving model
solvers (etc), all files are run-able.  Still some that should be maybe
be changed.  In particular, probably need to decide on a suffix to
replace the "gothic" in variables like gothicvP.

* Changed simulation variables names

Forgot to change the variable names in simulating and
shock-history-generating methods and functions for previous commit, now
fixed.  SolvingMicroDSOPs and cstwMPC (the files that rely on
simulation) both run.

A bunch of variables in cstwMPC still need to be renamed, but this can
wait until the simulation is revamped in the near future (the "effective
interest rate" as permanent shock needs to come out).

* Fixed problems with ENDG and Markov

The ENDG solver had a "hidden problem" that could cause weirdness at the
bottom of a consumption function in limited circumstances.  The issue
stemmed from the construction of the grid of aRto; there was also a
small error concerning MPCmax of the constrained vs unconstrained
function.

Also fixed some significant problems with the Markov solver; some of
these are long-standing, some were introduced in the OOP refactoring.
Solver now correctly computes human wealth, MPCmax, etc.  Needs more
testing, so I might split the MarkovType example into its own file with
other test cases.

* Perfect foresight solver fixed

The perfect foresight solver is now (mostly) working correctly.  The
previous version only solved infinite horizon problems with a single
period in the cycle, immediately jumping to the analytic solution.  The
new version works like the other solvers, doing one period at a time; in
an infinite horizon model, it iterates until convergence.

Three things to consider adding to perfect foresight:
1) LivFac.  In order to have the perfect foresight model match its risky
brethren, the user needs to adjust DiscFac to account for LivFac.
Should just add LivFac.
2) vFunc: Calculated incorrectly in this version, need to fix.
3) IncBool: Add a boolean to indicate whether income (of 1) is / will be
received.  It's possible to have a perfect foresight model where you
foresee a sequence of being paid and not being paid.

* Extended Markov: variable PermGroFac and Rfree

The Markov solver can now handle discrete states with different
permanent income growth factors and interest rates.  A new file has been
added with three more Markov examples to test the new functionality,
MarkovExamples.py.

There might still be bugs in the code/math, particularly with the
limiting MPCs.  Working on it.

* Many ConsumptionSavingModel (and core) improvements

Assorted changes and fixes as previously planned:

- HARKcore now has a Solution class that individual model solutions can
inherit from if they want.  Its only feature is a "default distance"
method (which can be overwritten, obv).  To use the method, the Solution
subclass merely needs to have an attribute called convergence_criteria,
which lists the attributes that must be sufficiently close for an
infinite process to be considered "converged".

- The distance method of ConsumerSolution has been deleted and replaced
with self.convergence_criteria = ['cFunc'], which does the same thing.
You can see how human wealth matters in infinite horizon models by
adding 'hRto' to convergence_criteria.

- The perfect foresight solver now takes LivFac as an input, consistent
with all other models in its module.

- The Markov solver generalization is now working correctly in seemingly
all cases.  It can deal with the interest rate, permanent income growth
factor, and income distribution varying across states; now correctly
handles MPC limits and borrowing constraints in oddball cases.

- MPC limits and human wealth are always computed now, rather than only
when CubicBool or vFuncBool are True.  However, they are not actually
used unless one of those is true.

- The "exogenous grid" solver has been deleted, per agreement (along
with the "hybrid type" example).  It can be re-added or rewritten later
(in OOP format), but was hopelessly outdated at this point.

- Value function construction now seems to work correctly in all models
in ConsumptionSavingModel: perfect foresight, risky income, kinked R,
and Markov (even in oddball cases).  It's a bit slow right now.

- The distance method of CubicInterp now depends on dydx values at nodes
(along with x and y values), and does not depend on extrapolation
choice.

* Completed Namingway

Made revisions to variable names in ConsumptionSavingModel.py (etc), per
meeting on 5/5/2016.  Brought variable names in TractableBufferStock.py
in line with ConsumptionSavingModel (but a few weird ones are left).
Also fixed nearly all variable names in ConsPrefShockModel, but some
changes might be warranted; will be done when this model gets its OOP
refactoring.

Various small changes to reconcile DCL-dev-1 and MNW-dev-2 merges:

- Reverted p_zero_income change, manually fixed the kappa_max issue.

- Made MarkovSolver accept transition_array as an input (was actually

d27df6cf7524c393790a9c031cc7db4eac97a7da0c9379f8a26aeadff8c2ff5264a971c3536aa68aMatthew N. Whitemnwhite@gmail.com2016-04-27T14:33:56-04:00MNW-dev-2 (#35)

* PrefShockCons solver working

Completed the solver for the preference shock model (with kinky R when
borrowing).  Only works for some parameters, is unable to solve for
others; possible numeric instability.

Also wrote shock history creator and simulator.  It runs, but not
correctly (average wealth grows without bound, even though there is
obviously a target wealth).  Even weirder, the simulation results look
correct if I swap the order of inputs (c,eta) for the consumption
function.  No, that doesn't make sense.

* Small ConsPrefShock changes + kernel regression

Twiddled the ConsPrefShock solver and simulator a bit, so that
simulation now works.  Maybe it always worked, and there wasn't a target
wealth when I thought there was.

Also added (but have not tested) a basic kernel regression method to
HARKutilities; need data on other computer, thus the commit.  Should add
kernels other than Epanechnikov and generalize kernelRegression to allow
this.

* Fixed lognormal, Biotech progress, ConsPrefShock working

HARKutilities incorporates a fixed version of Luca's closed form
lognormal approximator.  Biotech solver and simulator complete and
mostly tested; has multithreading capability.  And the consumption
preference shock model has been overhauled to work for (basically) all
parameter sets now.

* Lognormal tail augmentation, Biotech research fix

calculateLognormalDiscreteApprox now includes the option for "augmented"
tails.  User chooses the size of each tail (in terms of CDF), the number
of points, and the "order" of probability scaling; no tail by default.
Greatly increases accuracy in the preference shock model with only a
small number of discrete points.

Also fixed a serious math error concerning belief updating in Biotech.
Now works correctly, and value function does not converge.  Hooray?

3b7437819c84db2045b511495c27dd19e625b7ace794efd4b2b922569d75d33d7b24b18408be4272Matthew Whitemnwhite@gmail.com2016-02-01T14:40:46-05:00HARK alpha 1.1

An updated version with new functionality and small fixes written
between 12/16/15 and 1/31/16:

- Lots of new interpolation methods in HARKinterpolation.py, with demos
and testing in InterpTest.py.  Curvilinear2DInterp lacks derivative
methods, will fix.

- Fairly functional multithreading, with some serious overhead.  See
demo in MultithreadDemo.py.

- A new consumption-saving solver for models with exogenous transitions
between Markov states; demo'ed in ConsumerExamples.py and
TBSexamples.py.  Extrapolation issue seen in the latter is due to cFunc
converging way before gothic_h converges (in infinite horizon case).
Working on a fix that applies correct kappa_min and gothic_c in
postSolve(), coming soon.

- Experimental/trial/broken version of continuous-state-discrete-choice
solver, as in Rust-Schjerning-Iskhakov-Jorgens (2015).  It's best if
everyone ignores DiscreteChoice.py and DCtest#.py for now, they're a
mess and I'm going to start over.
